Experiments Overview

Directory structure, configuration files, feature store, hash versioning & reproducibility

Introduction

The experiments/ directory is the single source of truth for every artifact produced during an exploration-to-production run of the Customer Retention ML Framework. It stores:

  • Findings — YAML files that capture what the framework learned about each dataset (column types, quality issues, temporal configuration, multi-dataset relationships).
  • Recommendations — Per-layer transformation instructions (Bronze null handling, Silver scaling, Gold encoding) consumed by the generated pipeline.
  • Data layers — Delta Lake tables progressing from Landing → Bronze → Silver → Gold, plus scoring outputs (predictions & SHAP values).
  • Artifacts — Fitted transformers (scalers, power transforms) versioned by a content hash so they can be reproduced or rolled back.
  • Feature store — Feast-managed feature repository with materialized Gold-layer features, version-tagged with the recommendations hash for training/serving consistency.
  • MLflow tracking — Model registry, run metrics, and registered model artifacts.
  • Point-in-time snapshots — Frozen training data with cutoff dates for leakage-safe reproducibility.

This page embeds every configuration file produced during this run and explains how the versioning system ties everything together.

Directory Structure

experiments/ ├── artifacts/ │ └── 4131c25b/ ← content-hash versioned │ ├── manifest.yaml ← maps columns → fitted transformers │ ├── dow_cos_power_transformer.joblib │ ├── opened_mean_all_time_scaler.joblib │ ├── opened_sum_all_time_power_transformer.joblib │ ├── send_hour_mean_all_time_scaler.joblib │ ├── time_to_open_hours_count_all_time_power_transformer.joblib │ └── time_to_open_hours_sum_all_time_power_transformer.joblib ├── data/ │ ├── landing/ ← raw ingestion (Delta Lake) │ │ └── customer_emails_aggregated/ │ ├── bronze/ ← null handling, type fixes │ │ └── customer_emails_aggregated/ │ ├── silver/ ← scaling, normalization │ │ └── silver/ │ ├── gold/ ← encoding, feature-ready │ │ └── gold/ │ └── scoring/ ← predictions & SHAP │ ├── feature_importance.csv │ ├── predictions.parquet │ └── predictions_with_shap.parquet ├── feature_repo/ ← Feast feature store (local backend) │ └── data/ │ └── customer_churn_features/ ← materialized Gold features (Delta Lake) │ ├── *.snappy.parquet │ └── _delta_log/ ├── findings/ │ ├── snapshots/ ← point-in-time training data │ │ ├── training/ │ │ └── training_v1_metadata.json │ ├── unified/ ← joined multi-dataset view │ ├── point_in_time_registry.json │ ├── multi_dataset_findings.yaml │ ├── recommendations.yaml │ ├── *_findings.yaml ← per-dataset column findings │ └── *_recommendations.yaml ← per-dataset transform recommendations ├── docs/ ← exported HTML notebooks ├── mlruns/ ← MLflow tracking & model registry │ ├── artifacts/ │ └── models/ └── notebook_progress.json ← last executed notebook

Exploration Process & Output Files

The exploration notebooks run sequentially and each stage appends to the experiments directory. The table below shows the order in which files are created and what each one records.

Step Notebook Output File What It Records
1 00 Start Here point_in_time_registry.json
training_v1_metadata.json		 Freezes raw data at a cutoff date. Creates a leakage-safe training snapshot with row counts, column list, and data hash.
2 01 Data Discovery customer_emails_408768_findings.yaml Profiles the raw event-level dataset: column types, distributions, quality issues, temporal patterns, and aggregation config.
3 01a–01d Temporal customer_emails_408768_aggregated.parquet Aggregates events into entity-level features across time windows (180d, 365d, all_time). Output is one row per customer.
4 02 Column Deep Dive customer_emails_408768_aggregated_846212_findings.yaml Profiles the aggregated entity-level dataset: 72 columns, quality scores, distributions, per-column recommendations.
5 03–06 Quality,
Relationships,
Features		 customer_emails_408768_aggregated_846212_recommendations.yaml Accumulates transformation instructions across Bronze (nulls, outliers), Silver (derived features, interactions), and Gold (encoding, scaling, feature selection).
6 05 Multi Dataset multi_dataset_findings.yaml Registers all datasets, their relationships, the primary entity dataset, and temporal configuration.
7 10 Spec Generation recommendations.yaml Top-level silver layer config: entity column, join instructions, and cross-dataset aggregations.
8 Pipeline Execution artifacts/4131c25b/manifest.yaml Fitted transformers (scalers, power transforms) registered in a content-hash-versioned directory.
9 Pipeline Execution data/ layers + scoring/ Delta Lake tables (Landing → Bronze → Silver → Gold) and final predictions with SHAP values.
10 Pipeline Execution feature_repo/data/customer_churn_features/ Gold-layer features materialized to Feast feature store as a Delta Lake table. Version-tagged with v1.0.0_4131c25b for training/serving consistency.

Configuration Files

Every file below is produced automatically by the exploration notebooks. They are not meant to be hand-edited — they serve as a machine-readable track record of what the framework discovered and decided. The scrollable blocks show the complete file contents.

point_in_time_registry.json findings/ — 12 lines
Maps each dataset to its training snapshot. The cutoff_date defines the temporal boundary — no data after this date was used for training. The source_path and row_count let you verify you are working with the exact same input.
{
  "snapshots": {
    "customer_emails": {
      "dataset_name": "customer_emails",
      "snapshot_id": "training_v1",
      "cutoff_date": "2022-09-26T02:24:00",
      "source_path": "../tests/fixtures/customer_emails.csv",
      "row_count": 74842,
      "created_at": "2026-02-02T08:00:39.492143"
    }
  }
}
training_v1_metadata.json findings/snapshots/ — 29 lines
Detailed metadata for the frozen training snapshot. The data_hash is a fingerprint of the actual parquet bytes — compare it to verify nothing has changed. The feature_columns list shows the raw columns before any aggregation or engineering.
{
  "snapshot_id": "training_v1",
  "version": 1,
  "created_at": "2026-02-02T08:00:39.335561",
  "cutoff_date": "2022-09-26T02:24:00",
  "label_availability_filter": "label_available_flag == True AND feature_timestamp <= cutoff",
  "row_count": 74842,
  "column_count": 16,
  "data_hash": "51053360611aa638",
  "feature_columns": [
    "email_id",
    "entity_id",
    "sent_date",
    "campaign_type",
    "opened",
    "clicked",
    "subject_line_category",
    "send_hour",
    "device_type",
    "bounced",
    "time_to_open_hours",
    "unsubscribe_date"
  ],
  "target_column": "target",
  "timestamp_config": {
    "cutoff_date": "2022-09-26T02:24:00",
    "label_available_only": true
  }
}
customer_emails_408768_findings.yaml findings/ — 1136 lines
Event-level profiling of the raw dataset (74,842 rows × 16 columns). Organized as: header (source, row/column counts), then a columns: map where each column has inferred_type, universal_metrics (nulls, distinct counts), type_metrics (type-specific stats), quality_issues, and transformation_recommendations. The metadata: section at the end captures temporal patterns, recency analysis, momentum features, and aggregation config.

Feature Store Integration

After the Gold layer is built, the framework publishes features to a feature store so that training and inference always read from the same source with identical transformations applied. The framework supports two backends — Feast for local development and Databricks Feature Engineering for production — both accessed through a unified FeatureStoreManager interface.

Data Flow

Gold Layer
feature-ready data
→
publish_features()
FeatureStoreManager
→
Feature Store
Feast / Databricks
→
get_training_features()
get_online_features()

The publish_features() call writes Gold-layer data into the feature store as a versioned table. Downstream, get_training_features() retrieves point-in-time correct features for model training, while get_online_features() serves the latest values for real-time inference.

Dual Backend Architecture

Feast (Local) Databricks (Production)
Storage Delta Lake parquet files under experiments/feature_repo/data/ Unity Catalog managed tables (catalog.schema.table)
Online store SQLite (data/online_store.db) Databricks Online Tables
Registry SQLite (data/registry.db) Unity Catalog
PIT joins Manual temporal join via PointInTimeJoiner Native FeatureLookup with timestamp_lookup_key
Selection Default (auto-detected when not on Databricks) Auto-detected via is_databricks() environment check

Feast Configuration

The generated pipeline creates a Feast feature repository under generated_pipelines/local/customer_churn/feature_repo/. The two key files define the project settings and the feature view that maps to the materialized data.

feature_store.yaml feature_repo/ — 9 lines
Feast project configuration. The provider: local setting selects file-based storage. The offline_store: file reads directly from parquet, while online_store: sqlite provides low-latency key-value lookups for real-time serving.
project: customer_churn
registry: data/registry.db
provider: local
online_store:
  type: sqlite
  path: data/online_store.db
offline_store:
  type: file
entity_key_serialization_version: 2
features.py feature_repo/ — 36 lines
Auto-generated Feast definitions. The Entity declares customer_id as the join key. The FileSource points to the materialized parquet data. The FeatureView ties them together with a 365-day TTL and critically includes tags that embed the recommendations_hash (4131c25b) and version (v1.0.0_4131c25b) — linking this feature set to the exact pipeline configuration that produced it.
"""Feast Feature Definitions for customer_churn

Auto-generated feature view definitions for training/serving consistency.
Feature version: 4131c25b
"""
from datetime import timedelta
from feast import Entity, FeatureView, Field, FileSource
from feast.types import Float32, Float64, Int64, String

# Entity definition
customer = Entity(
    name="customer",
    join_keys=["customer_id"],
    description="Primary entity for customer_churn pipeline"
)

# File source pointing to materialized features
customer_churn_features_source = FileSource(
    path="data/customer_churn_features.parquet",
    timestamp_field="event_timestamp"
)

# Feature view definition
# Note: Features are dynamically determined from the parquet file schema
# This is a placeholder that gets populated when feast apply is run
customer_churn_features = FeatureView(
    name="customer_churn_features",
    entities=[customer],
    ttl=timedelta(days=365),
    source=customer_churn_features_source,
    tags={
        "pipeline": "customer_churn",
        "recommendations_hash": "4131c25b",
        "version": "v1.0.0_4131c25b"
    }
)

Version Tagging & Traceability

The Feast FeatureView tags are the bridge between the feature store and the hash versioning system. When a model is trained, the feature view's recommendations_hash tag records which recommendations produced the features. This means you can always trace from a trained model back to the exact feature definitions, transformations, and raw data that went into it.

tags = {
    "pipeline":             "customer_churn",
    "recommendations_hash": "4131c25b",          ← same hash as artifacts/4131c25b/
    "version":              "v1.0.0_4131c25b"     ← semantic version + hash
}

Point-in-Time Correctness

Both backends enforce temporal consistency to prevent data leakage:

  • Cutoff date validation — all feature tables published in a session must share the same cutoff date from the PointInTimeRegistry. A mismatch raises a validation error.
  • Temporal filtering — get_training_features() accepts an entity DataFrame with an event_timestamp column and only returns features where feature_timestamp ≤ event_timestamp, then picks the latest valid record per entity.
  • Data hash verification — on the Feast backend, a SHA-256 hash of the feature data plus cutoff date is stored in metadata. Reloading verifies the hash to detect tampering or drift.

Hash Versioning

Every pipeline run produces a content-addressed artifact set. The hash is derived from the recommendations file, ensuring that identical transformation instructions always map to the same artifact directory.

recommendations.yaml
+ per-dataset recs
→
SHA-256
first 8 chars
→
4131c25b
→
artifacts/4131c25b/
Version Tag
MLflow Run
Feature Store Tags

Where the Hash Appears

Location Example Purpose
Artifact directory experiments/artifacts/4131c25b/ Groups all fitted transformers for this run
Manifest paths artifacts/4131c25b/dow_cos_power_transformer.joblib Each entry in manifest.yaml references the hash directory
Version tag v1.0.0_4131c25b Semantic version + hash suffix for human & machine readability
Feast FeatureView tags recommendations_hash: 4131c25b Links feature store data to the pipeline that produced it
MLflow model registry Tag or run parameter Links a trained model back to its exact preprocessing pipeline
Training metadata data_hash: 51053360611aa638 Separate hash for the training data snapshot (in training_v1_metadata.json)

Version Tag Format

The full version tag follows the pattern:

v<major>.<minor>.<patch>_<hash>

  v        — version prefix
  1.0.0    — semantic version (major.minor.patch)
  _        — separator
  4131c25b — first 8 characters of SHA-256(recommendations)

If the recommendations change, the hash changes — making it immediately obvious that the artifact set is different, even if the semantic version was bumped manually.

Reproducibility Guide

Going Back in Time

Every training run is pinned to a point-in-time snapshot. The point_in_time_registry.json records the exact cutoff date and source data used.

  1. Check the snapshot registry Open experiments/findings/point_in_time_registry.json to find the cutoff_date and source_path for any snapshot.
  2. Verify data integrity Compare the data_hash in training_v1_metadata.json against a fresh hash of the source file to confirm nothing has changed.
  3. Load the frozen snapshot The parquet files under findings/snapshots/training/ contain the exact rows used for model training, filtered by the cutoff date.

Re-running from a Checkpoint

  1. Identify the pipeline stage Each data layer (Landing → Bronze → Silver → Gold) is stored as a Delta Lake table. You can restart from any layer without re-running upstream stages.
  2. Re-apply transformations The manifest.yaml in the artifact directory lists every fitted transformer. Load the joblib files to apply the same transformations to new data.
  3. Re-train with identical config The recommendations YAML files define every transformation. Re-running the generated pipeline with the same recommendations produces the same artifact hash.

Comparing Versions

  1. Diff the recommendations Compare recommendations.yaml files across runs. Different recommendations produce different hashes, making it easy to spot which transformations changed.
  2. Compare MLflow runs Use the MLflow tracking database at experiments/mlruns/mlruns.db to compare metrics (AUC, precision, recall) across model versions.
  3. Inspect artifact manifests Each hash directory has its own manifest.yaml. Diff two manifests to see exactly which transformers changed between versions.

Full Traceability Chain

Raw CSV → point_in_time_registry.json → training_v1_metadata.json → Landing → Bronze → Silver → Gold → artifacts/4131c25b/ → Feature Store (v1.0.0_4131c25b) → MLflow model → scoring/predictions.parquet

At every stage, the file on disk is the proof: the registry pins the data, the metadata pins the snapshot, the Delta tables pin each layer, the hash pins the transformers, the feature store tags pin the feature version, and MLflow pins the model. No step depends on external state outside this directory.
